Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Lately I can’t seem to keep my actions in order at work—I start doing things that don’t fit what I’m supposed to be doing and everything just turns into a mess. It's just really stressful because I want to do a good job, but I keep getting so mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a brain injury, stroke, seizure disorder, or another neurological illness before these problems started? I also haven't noticed any specific pattern of it happening more when I'm stressed or when other people are around.\n\nDoctor: Right before these episodes started, was there a major conflict, trauma, or stressful event, and do the episodes ever seem to help you avoid work or get extra attention or support? Also, when the episode ends, do you remember it clearly the whole time?\n\nPatient: I don't really have \"episodes\" that start and end, as this has just been a constant struggle for me lately. Because of that, I don't have times where I forget what happened, and it definitely doesn't help me avoid work or get any extra support. I'm not really sure about those other things you mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or feeling that people are controlling your movements or thoughts? Also, have others noticed you staying still in unusual postures for a long time or repeating words or movements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices and I've never felt like someone else is controlling my thoughts or movements. But people have definitely noticed me suddenly stopping and standing still in weird positions, like when I'm holding the pallet jack. I also do odd things like pacing back and forth or making faces without really knowing why.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly stop or hold a strange posture, how long does it usually last, and if someone asks you to move or guides you, can you respond normally or do you stay stuck? Do you ever repeat other people’s words or movements?"

When mounting problems at work led to a formal performance warning and his pastor mentioned being concerned about how disorganized and out of step he seemed during volunteer shifts, his parents brought him to psychiatric services for an urgent evaluation.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I can’t seem to keep my actions in order at work—I start doing things that don’t fit what I’m supposed to be doing and everything just turns into a mess.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When people at work or my parents ask me a simple question, like what I did on my last shift, I start talking about the pallets, then jump to a story about fishing with my uncle, then remember something about the safety video and I just keep going off in different directions until I can see on their faces that they have no idea what I’m talking about—even I lose track of what I was trying to say in the first place.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): On the floor I’ll suddenly stop in the middle of moving a pallet and just stand there holding the jack in a weird way or start re-stacking boxes that were already fine, or I’ll pace up and down the aisle waving my hands or making faces without really knowing why, even when my supervisor is telling me to knock it off and get back to the actual job.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because of how mixed up I get, my boss wrote me up for poor performance, my hours have been cut back, and even at church and at home people avoid putting me in charge of things because I can’t follow through the way I used to.\n- duration: All of this has been going on ever since I left community college for the full-time warehouse job, which has been close to a year now.\n- All of these difficulties seemed to start and get a lot worse after the stressful transition of leaving college to work full-time at the warehouse.\n\nDoctor: Besides getting mixed up at work, have you been feeling anxious or worried most days about a lot of different things, and is it hard to control that worry? Do you also notice symptoms like feeling on edge, muscle tension, trouble sleeping, or irritability?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have constant worry about different things, and I haven't noticed muscle tension or irritability. It's more that I just get really confused and disorganized with my actions, which is what's making everything so messy.\n\nDoctor: When this happens at work, do you know what you want to do but your body seems to do the steps in the wrong order, or is it more that anxiety makes you freeze or panic? Can you give me one specific example?\n\nPatient: It's not anxiety or panic at all, but my actions just get completely jumbled and out of order. For example, I'll suddenly stop while moving a pallet and just stand there holding the jack in a weird way, or start re-stacking boxes that were already fine. Sometimes I even start pacing up and down the aisles and waving my hands without really knowing why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you ever lose the ability to move part of your body or have weakness, tremors, or trouble walking, or is it mainly the odd, disorganized movements and postures? Have you ever become unable to speak or had changes in vision or sensation during them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any weakness, tremors, or trouble seeing or speaking. It's really just those odd, disorganized behaviors, like suddenly freezing in a weird posture while holding the pallet jack, or pacing and waving my hands. My body physically works fine, but I just end up doing these completely out-of-place actions.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you feel aware of what you’re doing but unable to stop or control it, or does it feel automatic or unreal, like it’s happening without your control? Do they tend to start more when you’re under stress or when other people are around?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain how it feels in the moment, and I’m not really sure if it feels unreal or just completely out of my control. I also haven't noticed any specific pattern of it happening more when I'm stressed or when other people are around.\n\nDoctor: Right before these episodes started, was there a major conflict, trauma, or stressful event, and do the episodes ever seem to help you avoid work or get extra attention or support? Also, when the episode ends, do you remember it clearly the whole time?\n\nPatient: I don't really have \"episodes\" that start and end, as this has just been a constant struggle for me lately. Because of that, I don't have times where I forget what happened, and it definitely doesn't help me avoid work or get any extra support. I'm not really sure about those other things you mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or feeling that people are controlling your movements or thoughts? Also, have others noticed you staying still in unusual postures for a long time or repeating words or movements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices and I've never felt like someone else is controlling my thoughts or movements. But people have definitely noticed me suddenly stopping and standing still in weird positions, like when I'm holding the pallet jack. I also do odd things like pacing back and forth or making faces without really knowing why.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly stop or hold a strange posture, how long does it usually last, and if someone asks you to move or guides you, can you respond normally or do you stay stuck? Do you ever repeat other people’s words or movements?\n\nPatient: I do sometimes stop and hold those odd positions, like with the pallet jack, even when my supervisor is telling me to get back to work, but I'm not really sure how long it actually lasts. I don't think I repeat other people's words or movements, though.\n\nDoctor: Outside of these odd postures and pacing, have you also had times of barely speaking, not moving unless pushed, refusing to eat or drink, or resisting when people try to reposition you? Have you noticed this happening for days at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never had anything like that happen. I don't have times where I stop talking, refuse to eat, or need people to physically push me to get me to move. It definitely has never lasted for days like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other schizophrenia-like symptoms, such as feeling people are watching or plotting against you, or your thoughts becoming jumbled or hard to follow? Before these movement problems started, did you notice a drop in motivation, emotions, or self-care?"

My body physically works fine, but I just end up doing these completely out-of-place actions.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you feel aware of what you’re doing but unable to stop or control it, or does it feel automatic or unreal, like it’s happening without your control? Do they tend to start more when you’re under stress or when other people are around?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain how it feels in the moment, and I’m not really sure if it feels unreal or just completely out of my control. I also haven't noticed any specific pattern of it happening more when I'm stressed or when other people are around.\n\nDoctor: Right before these episodes started, was there a major conflict, trauma, or stressful event, and do the episodes ever seem to help you avoid work or get extra attention or support? Also, when the episode ends, do you remember it clearly the whole time?\n\nPatient: I don't really have \"episodes\" that start and end, as this has just been a constant struggle for me lately. Because of that, I don't have times where I forget what happened, and it definitely doesn't help me avoid work or get any extra support. I'm not really sure about those other things you mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or feeling that people are controlling your movements or thoughts? Also, have others noticed you staying still in unusual postures for a long time or repeating words or movements?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices and I've never felt like someone else is controlling my thoughts or movements. But people have definitely noticed me suddenly stopping and standing still in weird positions, like when I'm holding the pallet jack. I also do odd things like pacing back and forth or making faces without really knowing why.\n\nDoctor: When you suddenly stop or hold a strange posture, how long does it usually last, and if someone asks you to move or guides you, can you respond normally or do you stay stuck? I don't think I repeat other people's words or movements, though.\n\nDoctor: Outside of these odd postures and pacing, have you also had times of barely speaking, not moving unless pushed, refusing to eat or drink, or resisting when people try to reposition you? I don't have times where I stop talking, refuse to eat, or need people to physically push me to get me to move. I also haven't noticed any drop in my motivation, emotions, or how I take care of myself.\n\nDoctor: How long have the disorganized behavior and jumbled thinking been going on, and have they been present most days for at least 1 month?
